#6896. It’s Time to Take Full Stock of Bond 26

Robert Pattinson is the new 007, with Denis Villeneuve directing and Steven Knight writing. From the villain to a possible release date, here’s what we know about Bond’s next chapter.

The search for the new James Bond was one of Hollywood’s most closely scrutinized casting processes. Producer Amy Pascal said the team was being “really, really methodical” in its search for Craig’s successor, and the shortlist of hopefuls was as long as Bond’s list of ex-flings, with about the same survival rate.

Josh O’Connor was an early favorite until he stepped back in October, saying he planned a career break in 2027; the Daily Mail later reported that he had turned the role down. Elsewhere, the betting and the gossip circled Slow Horses heartthrob Jack Lowden, Jacob Elordi, James Norton, Jack O’Connell, and Masters of the Air’s Callum Turner.

But after all that Bond-esque bluffing and counterbluffing from Amazon MGM Studios, Robert Pattinson was unveiled as the new 007 in mid-November. Everybody (mostly) breathed a sigh of relief—and turned their attention to the rest of the upcoming reboot. So, what do we know so far?

The second crucial element of Le Nouveau Bond is that French-Canadian director Denis Villeneuve will direct. He, too, has pedigree, not least his epic three-part Dune series, the first two films of which have already grossed more than $1 billion worldwide, with Part Three due out in December. “If there’s one thing we can be sure of with Denis, it’s that he will do something interesting with this,” a source within the film industry told me. “He is one of those directors who can turn even a bad script into a good film.”

Speaking of which, the other major name already signed up to the reimagining is Peaky Blinders creator Steven Knight. He will write the story.

Knight seems a natural fit for a darker, more character-driven Bond. His breakout was the screenplay for Dirty Pretty Things, which earned him an Oscar nomination. He went on to write David Cronenberg’s brutal Eastern Promises and the one-man thriller Locke.

But it was Peaky Blinders that made his name: a stylized, violent, brooding saga of a damaged man at the rotten heart of a criminal empire, which became a global hit for the BBC and Netflix. Knight, in other words, has previous when it comes to writing moody, morally murky men.

No need to say much—we know what we’re dealing with here. A man who makes good choices. A star.

At forty, Pattinson is a little older than Craig was when he got the job in 2005, aged thirty-seven. But the career behind him is hardly that of a conventional leading man. After the teen-idol years of Twilight, he spent a decade deliberately steering away from the obvious: Cronenberg’s Cosmopolis, the Safdie brothers’ Good Time, Robert Eggers’s The Lighthouse, and Claire Denis’s High Life, before returning to the blockbuster fold with Christopher Nolan’s Tenet, and The Batman.

He’s made stellar choices throughout his career, turning a heartthrob start into a credible run of good, risk-taking roles.

The first, most critical drop will, of course, be the villain. Because, as they say in Hollywood, a movie is only ever as good as its villain. Without a dastardly supervillain for Bond to shoot his PPK at, Bond is just a red flag in a black suit. No names have yet reared their heads. But that didn’t stop us from suggesting a few candidates.

Speaking of which, Amazon has shown no indication of a plan to depart from the Bond-girl tradition. Though it’s (thankfully) likely to be handled sensitively for 2026: expect a complex and fully realized love interest who will still be in a gorgeous dress by the second act.

Margaret Qualley has emerged as the bookies’ favorite, having already taken part in a Bond-tribute dance at the 2025 Oscars. Sydney Sweeney has said she’s up for joining the franchise, though she told Variety she’d have more fun playing Bond himself.

Anya Taylor-Joy, Florence Pugh, and Zendaya have also been linked, with the obvious advantage of having worked with Villeneuve on Dune.

They were most recently played, respectively, by Ralph Fiennes, Ben Whishaw, and Naomie Harris. It’s not known whether any of them will return to the roles, though history suggests it’s likely.

Craig’s run offers a rough template, for what it’s worth (which is not much). He was unveiled in October 2005, started shooting Casino Royale the following January, and had it in cinemas by November 2006. Pattinson’s unveiling comes just a few weeks later in the calendar, so a similar timetable would see cameras rolling early next year and a potential release date around the end of 2027. Though that feels like a tight turnaround for such a gargantuan project. Amazon will want to get this right, so given how long it strung out Pattinson’s casting, 2028 seems a more likely ballpark.

Here’s a boring-but-pertinent fact: three of Daniel Craig’s five James Bond films debuted in October: Quantum of Solace, Skyfall, and Spectre. The remaining two, Casino Royale and No Time to Die, were released in November and September, respectively.

But this is, of course, guesswork—Amazon has offered no indication that it will follow the Craig timeline, or anything else about the movie’s schedule.

This is perhaps the most interesting part of the whole process. It began in February 2025, when the Broccoli dynasty (led by long-serving Bond masterminds Barbara Broccoli and Michael G. Wilson) announced they were handing over creative control to Amazon MGM Studios (which was formed when Amazon bought Bond’s parent studio in 2022).

Within hours of the announcement, Amazon’s owner, Jeff Bezos—the world’s richest man—took to X to ask the world: “Who’d you pick as the next Bond?”

This followed rumors of a rift between the two parties, with the Wall Street Journal reporting that Barbara suggested to friends that she didn't like the idea of Amazon execs taking a data-driven approach to the series, as well as the use of the word “content” in creative meetings.

Just as Disney+ has produced many Star Wars spin-offs, Amazon MGM is expected to do the same with Bond—a process that began last year with the first TV series, 007: Road to a Million, a game show hosted by Succession’s Brian Cox.

Mike Hopkins, head of Prime Video and Amazon MGM Studios, said in February 2025: “We are honored to continue this treasured heritage, and look forward to ushering in the next phase of the legendary 007 for audiences around the world.”

#6887. The 25 Best Cocktails for Thanksgiving

With a whiskey warmer, spiced sour, or comforting classic in your hand, you can get through any awkward family gathering.

Are you ready for your annual awkward family gathering? Thanksgiving is finally here, and if thinking about safe topics to discuss with all three generations of your family is making you break out in hives, then rest assured we’ve got some recipes to give you the liquid courage to face it.

You’ve got the turkey basted and roasting in the oven. You’ve got your grandmother’s perfect mac and cheese. You’ve got mashed potatoes—the best. And you’ve got store-bought pie from your slacker cousin. But, wait, do you know what you’ll be drinking for the most important feast of the year? We promise: There’s something for everyone at your dinner table on this curated list of cocktails.

Does Grandpa only abide by the saying, “If it’s brown, drink it down?” We’ve got a few whiskey drinks that will keep him on ice. Did your aunt who drinks only French reds run through the bottle she brought already? There’s a sangria recipe that’ll have her too busy sipping to snip. Need a warm cup of something strong while you “take the dog for a walk”? There’s an Irish coffee that’ll keep your wits about you. So hurry up and add these cocktail recipes to your Turkey Day menu.

Here’s a whiskey sour that makes your fall-flavor dreams come true.

Add ingredients to a shaker and shake without ice. Pour into a glass and garnish with grated nutmeg and a dried apple slice.

This twist on the old-fashioned adds an autumnal flavor that will keep this classic feeling fresh.

Combine all ingredients into a mixing glass with ice. Stir until chilled and diluted. Strain into a rocks glass with a large cube. Garnish with an orange peel studded with cloves.

The delicious spice that goes into a pie doesn’t have to be just for lattes or baked goods.

Cover the rim of a stemless wineglass in simple syrup. Dip in cinnamon sugar and add ice to the glass. Pour in pumpkin-spice liqueur and Josh Cellars sauvignon blanc. Stir, then top off the glass with seltzer.

This three-ingredient cocktail is easy to whip up but will taste like it was complicated and time-consuming.

Mix cider and cinnamon whiskey in a Champagne flute. Top with Champagne, then stir lightly until incorporated. Garnish with an apple slice.

A sweet, fizzy sipper to enjoy before dinner.

Add all liquid ingredients to a stemless wineglass. Add ice. Stir to incorporate, then garnish.

A roasty, toasty espresso martini to enjoy after the big dinner.

Shake ingredients hard with ice until frothy, about 15 seconds. Double-strain into a chilled coupe or martini glass. Garnish with coffee beans, nutmeg, and cinnamon.

This sumptuous hot cocktail makes a great Thanksgiving treat.

In a mug, dissolve the sugar cubes in a little hot water. Add rum and butter. Fill the mug with hot water. Sprinkle nutmeg on top.

The margarita isn’t just a summer drink anymore.

In a cocktail shaker, combine all the ingredients with ice and shake. Rim a glass with cinnamon sugar and serve over rocks. Garnish with slices of lime and orange.

You need to keep the booze flowing to keep the mood light and the conversation going. So mix together this sangria recipe that’ll satiate any apple-cider lover.

Place apples, orange, and cloves in a large pitcher. Drizzle honey over the fruit, then add in apple cider, rum, ginger beer, and white wine. Stir to mix. Top with ice and let chill for 20 minutes before serving. Serve in glasses, including the chunks of fruit.

Whoever invented pouring bourbon into a cup of tea, we want to give you a big hug.

Boil water in a teapot. Steep star anise pod or tea bag inside. Add bourbon, honey, lemon, and Tiki bitters to a mug. Stir. Serve in glass mugs with a spoon and the teapot. To enjoy, pour tea over cocktail base to taste and stir.

This aperitivo is the perfect refreshment before you start serving those heavy Thanksgiving dishes.

Pour Campari and sweet vermouth into a highball glass filled with ice. Top with club soda. Garnish with an orange twist.

A balanced spin on the Manhattan that uses Scotch whisky, this cocktail will impress any fan of brown liquor.

Stir the Scotch, vermouth, and bitters well with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a lemon twist.

Simple enough to whip up in under five minutes, this cocktail has enough Scotch to knock Grandpa under the table.

Stir the Scotch and amaretto well with cracked ice in a mixing glass. Strain over fresh ice into an old-fashioned glass.

If you’re a big fan of cranberry sauce (the one that comes in a can), you can cure your cranberry craving with this vodka cocktail.

Shake ingredients in a shaker with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass, then garnish with lime.

This green-tinged cocktail tastes fresh and slightly tart, but it’s boozy enough to chill you out after that heavy holiday meal.

Add all ingredients into a shaker. Shake well with cracked ice. Strain into a coupe glass. Garnish with a lime wedge and Luxardo cherries.

The Black Russian is the espresso martini’s cooler, more laid-back cousin. It’s so simple to make and yet deliciously complex in taste.

Stir ingredients with ice in a mixing glass. Strain into an old-fashioned glass over fresh ice.

Sometimes you don’t want to have to choose between two options, because you want to have it all! And you can have it both ways, with this gin-and-vodka martini, the vesper.

Stir ingredients briskly with ice in a mixing tin until very cold. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a large, thin-cut lemon peel.

Why not turn your holiday gathering into an international experience? Incorporate this Russian classic into your cocktail rotation.

Squeeze lime juice into a copper mug (or a Collins glass) and drop in the spent hull. Add ice cubes, then pour in the vodka and fill with cold ginger beer. Serve with a stirring rod.

This rum cocktail will bring back fond memories of warmer weather.

Fill a tall glass with ice cubes. Add rum. Pour in ginger beer and lime juice. Stir with a barspoon, then garnish with a lime wedge.

Holidays are synonymous with Champagne, so pop open a bottle of bubbly and pour out one of these delicious cocktails.

#6884. Your Vagina Has a Party Trick, and It's Called Pompoir

Meet the sex technique that puts your pelvic floor muscles to work.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

If you have a vagina (or happen to be particularly fond of them), there's a decent chance you've heard of Kegels. You know, those pelvic floor exercises that involve squeezing and releasing muscles you might not think about all that often, but the second someone mentions them, you immediately start clenching (like right now).

But what if you could put those same muscles to work during sex? That's where pompoir comes in, using different rhythms and contractions to create new sensations during penetration, sometimes without moving your hips at all.

Sometimes referred to as vaginal gymnastics, pompoir takes things a step further than your standard Kegel routine. “Think of Kegels as going to the gym and pompoir as playing the sport,” says Karen Toubi, DO, a board-certified OB/GYN and menopause and vulvovaginal disorders specialist at Lavida Women's Health, Beverly Hills.

But before you start treating your vagina like it's training for the Olympics, there's something worth knowing: Pompoir isn't about permanently tightening your vagina, performing for your partner, or guaranteeing better orgasms. It's another way to explore pleasure, provided you're doing it safely and actually enjoying yourself.

Here's how pompoir works, how to try it, and what to know before giving it a go.

Unlike regular Kegels, pompoir is less about individual squeezes and more about controlling the timing, rhythm, and intensity of those contractions during sex. The idea is to create different sensations around a partner's penis or sex toy without relying entirely on thrusting.

The muscles doing the work are the same ones you engage during Kegels, including a group called the levator ani, which helps support your pelvic organs. And while you might be able to squeeze those muscles on command, getting them to cooperate in different rhythms takes a little more finesse. “Control comes with practice, like any motor skill,” says Liz Miracle, a pelvic floor physical therapist and head of clinical quality and education at Origin.

As for what it feels like? The person practicing pompoir might notice an internal pulsing sensation, while their partner could feel anything from a gentle squeeze to a fluttering movement. Of course, everyone's experience is different, and some partners might barely notice the contractions at all.

Before trying pompoir during sex, it helps to get familiar with your pelvic floor muscles on your own. Here's where to start.

Lie on your back with your knees bent and imagine you're trying to stop yourself from peeing or passing gas. Feel that gentle squeeze and lift? That's your pelvic floor doing its thing. Just don't actually practice by stopping your pee midstream, since that can interfere with normal bladder emptying.

“Your glutes, thighs, and belly should stay quiet,” says Miracle. Focus on squeezing and fully releasing the muscles without holding your breath. If you're struggling to figure out whether you're doing it correctly, a pelvic floor physical therapist can help.

Once you've identified the right muscles, try changing up the rhythm. Dr. Toubi suggests holding a contraction for up to 10 seconds before fully relaxing, or trying quick squeezes followed by an immediate release. You can also play around with different patterns, like two slow contractions followed by a faster one.

But don't focus solely on how hard you can squeeze. Amanda Neri, PT, DPT, pelvic health expert at Smile Makers Collection, emphasizes that learning to fully relax your pelvic floor between contractions is just as important as strengthening it. Otherwise, you risk creating unnecessary tension.

According to Bryony Lees, sex and relationships educator at Little Leaf Agency, missionary, cowgirl, and spooning can all be good positions for practicing pompoir, especially if you can keep your hips relatively still. Choose whichever feels most comfortable, and have your partner stay relatively still while you experiment with different squeezes and releases.

And yes, tell your partner what you're doing. Ask what feels good, pay attention to your own sensations, and don't feel like you need to master some elaborate routine to make it worthwhile. If anything hurts, stop.

It can, depending on the person. For the person practicing it, pompoir may increase body awareness and make certain sensations more noticeable. Some people also report stronger orgasms, since the pelvic floor muscles are involved in climaxing.

“For some people, yes, it can improve sex. It isn’t a guaranteed upgrade, though, and lots of people have great sex without ever thinking about this,” says Miracle. While research suggests pelvic floor muscle training can improve sexual function, pompoir specifically hasn't been studied enough to say whether it delivers the same benefits.

And despite what you might have heard, tighter doesn't automatically mean better. In fact, an overly tense pelvic floor can make sex painful. As Dr. Toubi explains, pompoir is about developing control, not permanently tightening your vagina or proving how good you are in bed. Reminder: Sex isn't a performance review.

Generally, yes, but that doesn't mean you should spend every spare minute squeezing your pelvic floor muscles. Overdoing it can lead to muscle tension, pelvic pain, urinary urgency, and even painful sex.

“A healthy pelvic floor needs to relax as well as squeeze,” says Dr. Toubi. If your muscles are already overly tense, adding more contractions could actually make things worse.

If you experience pain during sex, have vaginismus, chronic pelvic pain, or symptoms of pelvic organ prolapse, check with a pelvic floor physical therapist before trying pompoir. The same goes for anyone experiencing urinary leakage or other pelvic floor symptoms. If you've recently given birth or had pelvic surgery, talk to your healthcare provider about when it's safe to start.

Ultimately, pompoir should be something you try because you're curious and it feels good, not because you think your vagina needs improving. There are plenty of other ways to have great sex; no vaginal workouts required.

#6883. Research: Lack of Specialized Infrastructure Is a Top AI Adoption Challenge

Enterprise AI projects are running into infrastructure questions as organizations move from planning toward deployment, according to recent research Digital Realty. Forty percent of survey respondents cited a lack of specialized infrastructure required by AI as a main challenge to adopting a formal AI strategy, up from 9% in the company's 2024 research.

The Global Data Insights Survey 2026 points to a widening mix of infrastructure models behind enterprise AI. Half of respondents host AI models in private clouds, 86% are pursuing or exploring sovereign AI, 92% tie data-location strategy to their AI plans, and 88% follow a distributed-data approach.

The research is based on an online survey of 2,131 IT decision-makers across 19 countries, and 11 industries, conducted from Nov. 23, 2025, through Feb. 4, 2026.

Organizations also appear to be shifting from AI planning toward execution. The share actively executing AI to achieve operational efficiency or reduce costs rose from 27% in 2024 to 37% this year. Only 3% reported no measurable ROI to date, while 63% expect returns within the next six months to two years.

Specialized infrastructure led the survey's list of AI adoption challenges at 40%, followed by regulatory and personal-data concerns at 35%. Data availability and quality, storage capacity, interconnection, compute resources, and staffing also ranked as significant constraints.

Those requirements are also shaping workload placement. Integration with existing IT or data infrastructure ranked first at 43%, while regulatory compliance and performance were each cited by 37%. Data proximity, sovereignty, sustainability, cost, and intellectual property protection also factored into hosting decisions.

Private cloud features prominently across the report's AI hosting categories, but enterprises are clearly using a mix of private cloud, public cloud, on-premises infrastructure, and colocation. Private cloud accounted for 50% of custom-built AI model hosting and 47% for open source or fine-tuned large language models (LLMs).

Sovereignty is another part of that placement decision. Thirty-two percent consider sovereign AI — in which data and AI models remain within a single national or regulatory jurisdiction — a requirement, while another 54% are exploring it. The findings point toward hybrid environments in which infrastructure location, governance and data control increasingly overlap.

Microsoft has published a draft code of conduct that lays out how its homegrown AI models should behave, with human control taking priority over model capability, autonomy, and even task completion.

AI is changing which human capabilities matter most at work, yet employees are reporting that some of those same capabilities are being eroded, according to a new study from IBM.

Cisco has introduced Privileged Infrastructure Access, an approach to infrastructure administration that gives people and AI agents temporary, task-specific permissions instead of leaving standing privileges available after their work is done.

AI agents have moved into mainstream enterprise use, but the content infrastructure needed to support them has struggled to keep up, according to a new report from cloud content management company Box.

#6882. The 15 Most Thoughtful Personalized Gifts

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These will make shopping easier than ever this year.

It might only be November, but the holidays are just around the corner, and with Hanukkah starting early this year on December 4, I’m already collecting gifts for everyone I love. Getting ahead of the curve has its perks: fewer frantic shipping fees, for one, and enough time to find something that feels genuinely special.

The easiest way to make a gift special? Get it personalized. Because who wants to tear through wrapping paper only to find the same thing everyone else is getting this season? Go the custom route, and even a classic gift feels like it was made just for them (because, well, it was).

Think engraved ID bracelets and signet rings for the guys, birthstone bracelets and sparkly initial pendants for the women in your life, and personalized sports merch for the fan whose team is a whole personality trait. Add sentimental photo albums full of family memories and a few delightfully quirky surprises they’ll never see coming, and you’ve got a list worth getting a head start on. Here are some of our favorite personalized gift ideas for the holidays this year.

Know someone who takes great pleasure in writing hand-written letters? Make their next correspondence even more exciting with personalized writing paper from Smythson. Customize everything from the size and color of the paper, the text and motif at the top of the paper, and the tissue lining and border on the envelopes.

Engraved jewelry is one of the classiest personalized gifts you can get for someone, especially when it’s simple and refined like this ID chain bracelet from Miansai. I got this bracelet for my boyfriend with his name engraved on it for the holidays last year, and he wears it pretty much every day.

A personalized jersey is a no-brainer for anyone with a favorite sports team. The Fanatics custom shop has merch for NFL, MLB, NBA, WNBA, NHL, MLS, and even college teams, so it really has something for every kind of sports fan. And it’s not just jerseys—there’s also custom drinkware, bags, accessories, and more.

Even if your giftee isn’t super into astrology, they’ll appreciate a candle that’s just for them (or, well, anyone that shares their birthday). If they are into astrology, they’ll love all the fun facts about their birthday on the candle’s label, including their Zodiac sign, element, constellation, tarot card, and more. And all you have to know is their birthday, so it’s a pretty easy gift.

Speaking of birthdays, birthstone jewelry is another classic gift that’s personalized by design. And all you have to know is their birth month to get it right. Brilliant Earth has a great selection of birthstone jewelry, from pendant necklaces and colorful earrings to gemstone tennis bracelets and rings. You can even add multiple birthstones to this eternity lab diamond ring, so you can personalize it with the birthstones of everyone in your family.

For the one who spends their weekends on the golf course: They’ll truly impress their golf buddies with a box of personalized golf balls. And it comes with tees, a divot tool, and poker-chip ball markers so they have everything they need.

If you have the time and want to do something really special, order one of these luxury lighters from S.T. Dupont and get it engraved in person at a local engraving shop or jewelry repair store. The extra effort will show, and your giftee will have something to carry with them wherever they go for years to come.

You’ll have fun playing around with all the features in Ralph Lauren’s online custom hat maker—we certainly have. Choose the color and add a name, logo, or monogram, and the result will be a one-of-a-kind baseball cap your giftee will wear all the time.

Don’t just print out a photo and call it a day; get it printed on glass for something truly unique. Upload your photo to Fracture and personalize it even more with colorful borders, templates, frames, and stands.

Ever gone into a comic book store and seen the walls of Funko POP action figures from every franchise you can imagine? Having cute action figures of all your favorite Star Wars characters is great, but imagine how cool it would be to give someone an action figure of themself. With Funko POP, you can do just that—and you’ll have fun building it. You can even get a pair of Funko POP figures, so you can include yourself in it, too.

If you’re looking for a romantic personalized gift for your partner, surprise them with a watercolor painting of the place where you had your first date or the spot where you got engaged. Whether it was a local coffee shop, beloved movie theater, or beautiful outdoor space, it’ll look good painted above your names.

In a world where everything is online and in the cloud, a physical photo album is all the more sentimental and special. Artifact Uprising makes putting together a photo album easy, and the result is high quality and beautiful. It’s something they’ll want to display on their coffee table for everyone to see.

Gifts based on birthdays is a common theme in this gift guide, but what can we say? It’s an easy way to personalize stuff. If they’re a history buff (or just love reading the news), they’ll enjoy flipping through this customized book of newspaper headlines from their birthday through the years.

You know that person who is absolutely obsessed with their dog or cat? (Okay, it’s me.) They’ll obsess over any gift that has to do with their beloved pet, like this custom sweatshirt embroidered with the face of their dog or cat. Just upload a photo of their pet (you can probably find one on their Facebook or Instagram) and choose a sweater color and size.

A customized L.L. Bean tote bag is something they’ll use all the time. It’s one of our favorite gifts of all time, and it’s an absolute classic.
